At the 10th session held on 2 December 2008 and on 4 December 2008, the Tourism Committee of the Croatian Parliament discussed the Proposal for a Law on Tourist Communities and the Promotion of Croatian Tourism with the Final Proposal for a Law submitted to the President of the Croatian Parliament by the Government of the Republic of Croatia by Act of 27 November 2008, with a proposal to comply with Article 161. The Rules of Procedure of the Croatian Parliament shall adopt the proposed law by an urgent procedure. The final proposal for a law pursuant to Article 69 is discussed by the Committee on Tourism. The Rules of Procedure of the Croatian Parliament as a home working body, in particular in the area relating to the identification and monitoring of the implementation of the tourism development of the Republic of Croatia. On the basis of the preliminary exhibitors of the representatives of the proposings and representatives of the committee, it has been established that the Law on Tourist Communities and the promotion of Croatian tourism are... The financial laws he passed in 1991, adapted to this territorial structure of the Republic of Croatia in 1994, created assumptions for the construction and operation of the systems of tourist communities and national tourism organisations based on a long tradition and almost 150th anniversary of the first organisational forms in Croatian tourism. [[The speaker doesn't understand.]] During the 15-year activities in the Republic of Croatia, on the principles of non-profitability, not politics and democracy, as well as the work of general benefit, this system has shown all its importance and effectiveness in the field of promoting, improving and promoting our tourist offer. That during the implementation of the aforementioned laws, there has been an expression of some non-compliance of certain provisions in relation to the experience gained, and in particular the need for better coordination within the tourism community systems, more effective formulations of tourism promotion programmes and more transparent use of funds and better supervision of the financial operations of these communities. On the basis of the debate, it can be argued that there has been a huge dissatisfaction with the members of the board, as well as dissatisfaction from certain tourist communities because of the fact that this law, which is of great importance not only for the development of Croatian tourism but also for its positioning on the international tourist market, is brought into an urgent procedure. For this reason, the Committee has taken a position to refer the proposal to the applicant to consider the possibility of this law being adopted in a regular procedure so that a more high-quality debate can be achieved, and the different factors involved in the creation of a Croatian tourist product can express their opinions and suggestions. After a general debate, the Tourism Committee of the Croatian Parliament unanimously decided to propose to the Croatian Parliament to pass the Law on Tourist Communities and promote Croatian tourism with 8 amendments. If necessary, I will read them but we have them in writing. And since the other laws on residence tax and membership are being discussed together, I will read only the end that after the discussion of the Tourism Committee on the Law on Tourist Tax by majority of votes; 8 for and 1 against the decision to propose to the Croatian Parliament to adopt the Law on Tourist Tax with 18 amendments, and as regards the Law on Tourist Memberships, the Committee on Tourism unanimously decided to propose to the Croatian Parliament to adopt the Law on Tourist Memberships in Tourist Communities with 3 amendments. We all have amendments in the board's records. Thank you.

ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-09-0.u21974	Thank you, Mr. Vice President. My colleagues and colleagues, the Honorable Minister, the Secretary of State. So, before us, we have a three-point debate. These are the Final Proposal of the Law on Tourist Communities and the Promotion of Croatian Tourism, the Final Proposal of the Law on Memberships and Tourist Communities and the Final Proposal of the Law on residence tax. Therefore, the Croatian Tourist Board as a national tourist organisation was established to create and promote the identity and reputation of Croatian tourism, plan and implement a common strategy and concept of its promotion, propose and implement promotional activities in the country and abroad of common interest for all tourism entities and raise the quality of the entire tourist offer. The Act on Tourist Communities and the Promotion of Croatian Tourism and Follow-up Financial Laws, which was passed long ago in 1991, is adapted to the new territorial structure of the Republic of Croatia. '94. In the years, assumptions were created for the construction and operation of tourist communities and national tourism organisations based on a long tradition, almost 150th anniversary of the first organisational forms in Croatian tourism. Some of the most important tasks of the Croatian Tourist Board are to combine the entire tourist offer of the Republic of Croatia, develop programmes and plans to promote Croatian tourism products, encourage and coordinate the promotion of all tourist communities, as well as all economic and other tourism entities that act directly and indirectly to improve and promote tourism in Croatia, establish tourism representations and facilities abroad, organise and oversee their work. Thus, it should be mentioned that there are exposures in Austria, Belgium, the Czech Republic, Denmark, France, Italy, Japan, Hungary, the Netherlands, Germany, Poland, Russia, the United States, Slovenia, Spain, Sweden, Switzerland and the UK. He is working with national tourism organisations of other countries and specialised international regional tourism organisations. Based on the report from the tourist communities, the county monitors, analyzes and evaluates the implementation of plans and programmes of established tasks and the role of the tourist communities system, takes measures and activities to develop and promote tourism in the touristy underdeveloped parts of the Republic of Croatia. The proposal for a new Law on Tourist Communities expanded the number of tasks of tourist communities, and thus provided the possibility of creating a business policy of the tourist community, innovation, development of destination management in cooperation with local self-governments, counties and at the level of Croatia as a whole. As we know, the bodies of the Croatian Tourist Board are the Assembly, the Tourist Council, the Supervisory Board and the President. Croatia's tourism community president's duty is being handled by the tourism minister. The bodies are still the congregations, the tourist council, the supervisory board and the president of the tourist community. The Croatian Tourist Board operates on the basis of the annual work programme and the financial plan adopted by the Assembly and generates revenues from the following sources: residence taxes, membership fees, state budget and other sources. The tourist community in Croatia operates on the level of municipalities or cities, county or the Tourist Board of the City of Zagreb and the Croatian Tourist Board with a central center in Zagreb. So far, we also have examples of popular forms of tourist communities such as Krka is an island tourist community which is a unique example of uniting the interest of a more tourist community in the island area, with a view to better quality promotion and development of the tourist product, as well as an example of the Makarska Riviera Tourist Board. These are positive examples, and this amendment to the Law on Tourist Communities encourages and enables the establishment of these and similar forms of tourist communities. Incomes in tourist communities in 14 years of existence justified their existence. Tourist communities are important subjects of development and promotion of Croatian tourism. In everyday application of the existing Law on Tourist Communities, certain indeterminations, inaccuracy and inaccuracies to be corrected by this law have been observed, and the activities of tourist communities will be more effective and the spending of financial resources will be more meaningful. The aim is, with the least direct financial support from the budget, to provide a quality and efficient system for the tourism community system. This law lays down basic issues that are regulated, which is the structure of the systems of tourist communities, the bodies of tourist communities, the way of work, the tasks, the principles of financing, management, membership in tourist communities, income and the use of these incomes and supervision of the spending of these revenues. This will achieve a better quality tourist offer, development of tourism products, spending resources, promotion, coordination, professional staff and thus the efficiency of a system that counts in Croatia alone 330 tourist communities. Therefore, the implementation of this law does not require funding from the state budget, it is not planned to do so, and since the Law on residence tax is a law which is also in line with European Union rules, it is necessary to adopt all three laws together to ensure the regular functioning of the system. The Act on Tourist Communities and the Promotion of Croatian Tourism in Fundamental Provisions in Article 10(3) states that the tourist community is allowed to publish commercial ads on its promotional materials, sell souvenirs, tourist maps and guides, except for its own promotional material. This change of law is considered positive because the inability to sell souvenirs, tickets and so on. gave the impression that the tourist community does not care about the needs of guests who are in a place where they can get all the tourist information they want to buy souvenirs, tickets, etc. Also, commercial advertising that will certainly help tourist communities in the funded promotional material, and to interested members and partners the possibility of a quality presentation. All materials are printed in high loads so that this will enable mutual interest. Article 14(3) is amended and it is said that members or representatives of members of the tourist community cannot, through the power of authority, empower another person to participate in the work of the congregation instead. This stops the abuse of positions and voting in another's name, which we consider to be positive and important. Article 18(2) changes and says: "The duty of the president of the tourist municipality or of the city and county shall be exercised by the municipal chief, mayor or mayor." An extremely important and major change in the law, which improves the co-operation of the tourist community and local self-government, has happened in practice that the presidents of tourist communities, representatives of tourism companies who have usually indirectly filtered the ideas of administration through tourist communities. They imposed costs, guidelines for working towards the tourist community. With this change, the tourist community will cooperate more effectively on the city's projects, assist in co-financing, joint promotion and destination management, which is listed in this new law as one of the new tasks of the tourist community. We had examples of where in tourist communities the presidents, i.e. The members and president of the council or the assembly of the tourist community are even a representative of the company that in the meantime became the owner of a foreign person, meaning a foreign company. So we have a president of the tourist community who represents a foreign company, practically, a tourist house, which I do not think that this company will certainly represent the legitimate and quality public interest of a city or municipality. So this change is very important. With the fact that it is likely that some amendments will go so that perhaps some person who can be authorised by the mayor, mayor, or possibly the mayor to be on his behalf. In Article 19(1) of the amendment to the law, one member of the Board of Supervisors of the Tourist Board of the Tourist Board delegates the tourism council of the County. This wants to carry out a monitoring that is impartial, even more quality, thus reducing the potential for poor management. In Article 23 of the Director of the Tourist Office, professional workers shall be required to carry out the tasks of the tourist community, except for the fulfilment of the specific conditions laid down in the Rules of Procedure, to have a professional examination passed, which has not been the case so far. Also, Article 23 discusses a conflict of interest, and in practice it was very often possible to find examples where the directors of tourist communities or members of their families perform a tourist activity directly related to the work of the tourist community. In that case, there was the possibility of agreeing to certain decisions. The legislator, precisely because of these problems in practice, wanted to end this negative development. Article 30, paragraph 1 as voluntary members, introduces one quality, one novelty to which members of the tourist community in accordance with Article 28. This is a request from persons who wish to enable prominent individuals who wish to participate in the work of the assembly but who do not have a high enough payment of the residence fee to be able to participate in the work of the assembly. The law stipulates that, as so far, 65% of these funds belong to the tourist community of the municipality or city on whose territory the income is collected, 10% gets the county tourist community and 25% Croatian tourist community. The new news is that the tourist communities in which the collection of less than 200 thousand kuna on residence fees and the same amount on membership fees do not have to give that 25% of the money to the Croatian tourist community but are obliged to 20 % to be paid into the budget of the county tourist community and there remains 80% of the funds that we think are extremely important because it will make it important to make it easier for them to function. And what I want to say in conclusion is that we in HSS really expect this package of tourism laws as well as the laws that are passed in agriculture and the budget that we are going to talk about tomorrow to finally give us one space to connect green and blue fields. This has been said for many years, but there is no concrete indication of the desired results on the ground. I have already said several times on behalf of the HSS club that we in the HSS think that this crisis may be a chance to place a Croatian agricultural product in tourism because tourists spend 6 billion euros annually in Croatia. What we expect in HSS and what we would certainly like to see is that in those 6 billion euros spent by tourists in Croatia, most of that money is related to Croatian products, not Chinese souvenirs, beef from Argentina, milk powder from Bosnia and Herzegovina, vegetables from Spain or Greece, and so on or that the caterers simply did not have that adequate contact between the producers of agricultural products themselves and people who are engaged in hotel and catering are reaching out for large trade chains, consumables, lids, marbles or similar. So we think this is an excellent opportunity, that this package of tourism laws goes for the fact that Croatian product really is better placed in tourism. We believe in HSS that our producers can also produce enough quality agricultural products that meet standards and are certainly better than what is very often placed on our guests, and we also believe that this has been confirmed on the Days of Croatian Tourism in Opatija that our hosts and hotelers are also ready to give that hand to the community and that they are ready, with adequate government intervention, to link what we are constantly talking about and that is a blue and green field. Ministers wish you and your associates a lot of success because next year certainly brings many challenges not only for Croatian tourism, but above all for Croatian tourism. The HSS Club will support this package of laws and I believe that these proposals that we have made quite seriously on the integration of agriculture, the integration of tourism, the opening of the possibility that several hotels on the Adriatic are being placed and organic products produced from our rural households and agricultural products, that they will certainly find understanding in the Ministry of Tourism, but also in the Government of the Republic of Croatia, and that we will see concrete results in this respect next year. ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-09-0.u22000	Mr. President of the Croatian Parliament, ladies and gentlemen of the Parliament, the honourable representatives of the motion, Mr. Minister. Three legislative proposals that are before this House, the Proposal for a Law on Tourist Communities and the Promotion of Croatian Tourism, the Proposal for a Law on residence tax, the Proposal for a Law on Memberships in Tourist Communities, are linked to each other so that they really form a functional and legal package that prescribes the establishment and operation of a system of tourist communities. This is necessary for their compliance, and this means, at the same time, changes and additions, which in this case, given the numerous changes so far and the addition of existing laws with the right to choose the form of new legislative proposals in order to avoid the transparency and incomprehensibility of this very important legislation. As so far, tourist communities are legal persons in whom concepts meet and co-operate in the public and private sectors for the purpose of developing, acting and promoting Croatian tourism as important economic activities in which they pursue their interests. Mandatory forms are, according to Article 4 of the new legal proposal, the tourist community of the municipality or city, the tourist community of the area in which the tourist communities of municipalities and cities are not established, the tourist community of the City of Zagreb, the tourist community of the county and the Croatian tourist community as a roof organisation for the territory of the Republic of Croatia. But mandatory tourist communities are possible, the law predicts them but with the previous consent of the ministry and provided that the means of operation are provided by the municipality or city from their budgets. Mandatory members are also bonds of payment of membership fees, which are legal and natural persons who generate income by providing services in tourism or with tourism directly related activities. The amount of membership fees was determined by the draft membership laws in tourist communities and these tourist resorts are classified as so far in four classes A, B, C and D, with all settlements above 30,000 inhabitants now in class A. The rate of the membership fee depends on the activity group and the tourist class and on the rate of the total income. The tax administration is empowered to supervise the payment of membership fees to legal and physical persons, and the Ministry of Oversight in tourist communities. The bodies of the tourist community are a congregation of the tourist community of the municipality, city, etc. while the Assembly at national level is called the National Assembly of the Tourist Board. The tourist council is the executive body of the tourist community, the supervisory board also exists under this law-proposed structure. The president of the tourist community is a special institution, and the paper is that the president of the tourist community is in office, so not so far as the selection of members of the assembly but as mayor, or mayor. These have avoided the unsolicited situations that have occurred so far. The duplication between local self-government and the assembly of the tourist community and the inability and choice and functioning that actually the entire system has been holding back. The activities of tourist communities and in this as well as in the current law are based on general utility, but this law proposes and brings the exception of possible commercial action, and the tourist community can manage tourism infrastructure, organise events and events, conduct commercial ads in its votes, give souvenirs, tourist maps and guides, intercede in booking accommodation if there is no travel agency in this place. There is also an obligation of professional examination for working in the tourist office for the director, as well as for professional workers. The residence tax law determines how payments, conditions and obligations of this most issued income for the activities of tourist communities. They've been detained from the law so far by a good solution, by a pause for private renters, and by a vignette for boaters. And here, after 14 years of functioning of the tourism community system, it has been shown that this system is a good, animator and promoter of Croatian tourism, but the shortcomings that have also been identified have also been shown and this legislative proposal has tried to address these shortcomings. I think the solutions are good. I support the adoption of this law and I also support the urgent procedure in order to show the improvements it brings already in this next tourist season.
ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-09-0.u22001	Thank you. Ms. Representative Neda Klaric. Here you go.
ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-09-0.u22002	Thank you very much, well-respected President of the Croatian Parliament, dear Secretary of State, colleagues and colleagues. In order to strengthen and promote Croatian tourism, which represents a public interest as well as economic legal and physical persons, which is the interest of the private sector, tourist communities as a legal person have been established. The law requires mandatory but equally optional forms. The objectives are to encourage the development of an existing tourism product, as well as new products, to develop awareness of the importance and effects of tourism with particular emphasis on environmental protection to create good assumptions for tourism. The achievement of income from residence tax, membership fees, budgets, loans and voluntary revenues cannot be engaged, of course it is their main, i.e. the main basis of how they are financed, but also cannot deal with activities and which make direct profits, and cannot play a role in the core capital of a company. They can sell souvenirs, tourist tickets, guides for better quality services. But it is important to say that after 14 years of law enforcement, the system has proved many advantages, but the tourist communities have also strengthened and now it is time to work on a more rational structure and use of funds. Oversight should also be improved, better co-ordinated in the connection system of tourist communities, as well as the number of workers in tourist communities and the number of body members. The purpose of the new law is also to contribute to the overall quality of the tourist product, and the Croatian model of tourist communities is similar, very similar to the Austrian model. The law already said that the president of the tourist community becomes the mayor of the municipality, the mayor, or mayor, depends on the unit of local, or regional self-government for which it was founded. So far, it has been a person elected by the assembly on a proposal from the municipal or city chiefship, and the president of the county tourist community has been a member of the county headship in charge of tourism. And we think that this is a better solution now, because practice has shown that there have often been disagreements, because the assembly in a number of cases would disagree with the selection of the person proposed by the headship, which created major problems in the work and sometimes blocked the work of the tourist community. This ensures cooperation between the tourist community and the local unit, or regional self-government for the area in which it operates, and also eliminates the possibility of conflict of interest because the duties of directors and employees of the tourist office are incompatible, as well as the response to the previous discussion and the objection to fellow mayor Cappelli, and the supervisory board, as well as the director is prevented from carrying out tourist and catering activities in the area in which the tourist community is established. It also cannot take part in or participate in decisions affecting the financial or other interest of a spouse, child or parent, and the development of tourism must be given special attention to local self-government because it contributes to the greater quality of life of the population, as well as to economic development and cooperate with the tourism community to continue to develop successfully in this area. The point is to act on local and tourist communities and their resources to focus on improving the living conditions of tourism in the community. With this law, economic inspectors will be able to carry out more quickly, more effective inspection because it obliges the tourist community to verify and collect data on the application and check-out of tourist stays during the main tourist season, in order to calculate the payment and payment of the tourist tax. Tourist communities will manage the units of local self-government with public tourist infrastructure, and will participate in defining the policy and development of tourism, and this is also a very important quality at the level of the municipality or the city, to participate in creating conditions for better co-ordination of the public and private sector. There will also be a different assembly composition, so one member cannot have more than one third of the total representatives in the assembly, and thus prevents the power to decide individual entities and to ensure the rational use of funds. The composition of the tourist council, which will be held by the president and eight members from the ranks of members of the tourist community, is also changed, and so far this has not been the case. It is necessary to say that the new law gives greater authority to the Croatian tourism community, directly in relation to the powers so far, both in terms of supervision and in terms of concrete measures authorised to take in the exercise of supervision, which strengthens the system of problem-solving within the system itself. The membership fee is definitely a very significant income of tourist communities, so it is important, of course, is also important to schedule these funds within the complete system. A significantly smaller accommodation capacity in the continental part of the Republic of Croatia results in the fact that the share of the membership fee in the tourism community's income accounts for 80 to 90%, while in coastal counties, the tax is the most important source of income, ranging from 60% to 85% of these laws regulates the obligation to pay membership fees, basics and rates for the accounting and payment of tourist community, payment methods, records, billing and payment as well as the allocation of membership fee to the tourist community, in a way that is distributed with 65% of that tourist community, 10% to the tourist community of the county and 25% to the Croatian tourist community. The exception is made by those tourist communities, and this is the newspaper that we should all praise, which generate gross income less than 200 thousand kuna per year, so membership funds are allocated with 85% of that community and 15% to the county tourist community. ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-09-0.u22006	Thank you. Dear President, dear colleagues and colleagues, representatives of the government. Because of this injustice, the owners of such facilities and their families are placed in an unfair position in relation to all other taxpayers in the Republic of Croatia cannot support this bill. The Constitution of the Republic of Croatia states in Article 14, "All are equal before the law.". However, according to the Law on residence tax, it is not quite so because certain groups of citizens are unfairly taxed twice in this law. How? Simple. And that is ultimately recognised by the legislator himself, the tourism ministry, when in an unmistakable reasoning of the law, I quote: "Article 12 provides for the obligations of the owner of houses and holiday apartments. Tourist tax is essentially a fee for the use of general tourist services and benefits provided to visitors in the tourist municipality or city, which, by their own size and quality, cross the frameworks of the needs of the local population. The owners of houses and holiday apartments are not residents of the municipality or city in which the property is located and the reason for the acquisition is to meet their own tourist needs which they have estimated to be the most completely satisfied using the general tourist services and conveniences of the tourist municipality or the city in which the property is located. Accordingly, like other visitors in the tourist municipality or the city, they must pay a tourist tax.". It would all be nice and wonderful and no one would say a word against it if owners of such facilities would not pay all other fees and taxes throughout the year, which are paid by both persons living and registered in the area. And according to information from citizens and citizens who have contacted me on this bill all these other fees and taxes are not small at all. The explanation that these bonds or bonds are obliged or obliged to pay a residence tax because, again, I quote, "the incentive to acquire and real estate to meet their own tourist needs that they have estimated to be most fully satisfied using the general tourist services and the benefits of the tourist municipality or the city in which the property is located" is just incredibly cynical. In fact, it is these citizens and citizens that, by paying all taxes and fees per year for these facilities throughout the year, the facilities they use perhaps three months a year and probably significantly less generously finance these local self-governments, although their benefits are significantly less advantageous and thus significantly less negatively affecting the consumption of valuable resources. The proposer defends the irrefutable or double taxation of this group of debtors and the following non-stop words: "However, since the owners of houses and holiday apartments pay other public fees and real estate taxes which are the income of a tourist municipality or city in Article 12, it is prescribed that the owner of a house or apartment for rest and members of his family pay a residence tax reduced by 70% and the residence tax may also pay in a flat fee. Oh, please, how incredibly generous of the proposer. The very fact that the residence tax paid by this group of taxpayers or bonds is not excessively high especially if it is paid as a flat-rate and amounts to some 70 kunas does not change anything or that such a provision puts individual citizens and citizens of Croatia in an unequal position in relation to other individuals and thus unfairly discriminate. This bill provides that owners or property owners, if they wish to pay a flat-rate stay tax, must do so by 15 July of the current year at the latest, which is also absurd and discriminatory. Namely, if owners or property owners go to their house at the beginning of the 8th month if they want to pay a flat tax, what? One weekend at 6 or 7 months will have to run into a place where they have real estate in order not to miss the deadline for paying that fee. In this way, pausal is no longer any dating to owners and property owners because the way to a location where they have a property rarely costs 1000 kuna. For all the above, I conclude that this law has the elements of a violation of the Constitution of the Republic of Croatia because it unfairly discriminates against one group of citizens or citizens of the Republic of Croatia and therefore cannot support such an unfair bill.

ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-09-0.u22140	Thank you, Mr. Vice President. My colleagues and colleagues, the Honorable Minister, the Secretary of State. So, before us, we have a three-point debate. These are the Final Proposal of the Law on Tourist Communities and the Promotion of Croatian Tourism, the Final Proposal of the Law on Memberships and Tourist Communities and the Final Proposal of the Law on residence tax. Therefore, the Croatian Tourist Board as a national tourist organisation was established to create and promote the identity and reputation of Croatian tourism, plan and implement a common strategy and concept of its promotion, propose and implement promotional activities in the country and abroad of common interest for all tourism entities and raise the quality of the entire tourist offer. The Act on Tourist Communities and the Promotion of Croatian Tourism and Follow-up Financial Laws, which was passed long ago in 1991, is adapted to the new territorial structure of the Republic of Croatia. '94. In the years, assumptions were created for the construction and operation of tourist communities and national tourism organisations based on a long tradition, almost 150th anniversary of the first organisational forms in Croatian tourism. Some of the most important tasks of the Croatian Tourist Board are to combine the entire tourist offer of the Republic of Croatia, develop programmes and plans to promote Croatian tourism products, encourage and coordinate the promotion of all tourist communities, as well as all economic and other tourism entities that act directly and indirectly to improve and promote tourism in Croatia, establish tourism representations and facilities abroad, organise and oversee their work. Thus, it should be mentioned that there are exposures in Austria, Belgium, the Czech Republic, Denmark, France, Italy, Japan, Hungary, the Netherlands, Germany, Poland, Russia, the United States, Slovenia, Spain, Sweden, Switzerland and the UK. He is working with national tourism organisations of other countries and specialised international regional tourism organisations. Based on the report from the tourist communities, the county monitors, analyzes and evaluates the implementation of plans and programmes of established tasks and the role of the tourist communities system, takes measures and activities to develop and promote tourism in the touristy underdeveloped parts of the Republic of Croatia. The proposal for a new Law on Tourist Communities expanded the number of tasks of tourist communities, and thus provided the possibility of creating a business policy of the tourist community, innovation, development of destination management in cooperation with local self-governments, counties and at the level of Croatia as a whole. As we know, the bodies of the Croatian Tourist Board are the Assembly, the Tourist Council, the Supervisory Board and the President. Croatia's tourism community president's duty is being handled by the tourism minister. The bodies are still the congregations, the tourist council, the supervisory board and the president of the tourist community. The Croatian Tourist Board operates on the basis of the annual work programme and the financial plan adopted by the Assembly and generates revenues from the following sources: residence taxes, membership fees, state budget and other sources. The tourist community in Croatia operates on the level of municipalities or cities, county or the Tourist Board of the City of Zagreb and the Croatian Tourist Board with a central center in Zagreb. So far, we also have examples of popular forms of tourist communities such as Krka is an island tourist community which is a unique example of uniting the interest of a more tourist community in the island area, with a view to better quality promotion and development of the tourist product, as well as an example of the Makarska Riviera Tourist Board. These are positive examples, and this amendment to the Law on Tourist Communities encourages and enables the establishment of these and similar forms of tourist communities. Incomes in tourist communities in 14 years of existence justified their existence. Tourist communities are important subjects of development and promotion of Croatian tourism. In everyday application of the existing Law on Tourist Communities, certain indeterminations, inaccuracy and inaccuracies to be corrected by this law have been observed, and the activities of tourist communities will be more effective and the spending of financial resources will be more meaningful. The aim is, with the least direct financial support from the budget, to provide a quality and efficient system for the tourism community system. This law lays down basic issues that are regulated, which is the structure of the systems of tourist communities, the bodies of tourist communities, the way of work, the tasks, the principles of financing, management, membership in tourist communities, income and the use of these incomes and supervision of the spending of these revenues. This will achieve a better quality tourist offer, development of tourism products, spending resources, promotion, coordination, professional staff and thus the efficiency of a system that counts in Croatia alone 330 tourist communities. Therefore, the implementation of this law does not require funding from the state budget, it is not planned to do so, and since the Law on residence tax is a law which is also in line with European Union rules, it is necessary to adopt all three laws together to ensure the regular functioning of the system. The Act on Tourist Communities and the Promotion of Croatian Tourism in Fundamental Provisions in Article 10(3) states that the tourist community is allowed to publish commercial ads on its promotional materials, sell souvenirs, tourist maps and guides, except for its own promotional material. This change of law is considered positive because the inability to sell souvenirs, tickets and so on. gave the impression that the tourist community does not care about the needs of guests who are in a place where they can get all the tourist information they want to buy souvenirs, tickets, etc. Also, commercial advertising that will certainly help tourist communities in the funded promotional material, and to interested members and partners the possibility of a quality presentation. All materials are printed in high loads so that this will enable mutual interest. Article 14(3) is amended and it is said that members or representatives of members of the tourist community cannot, through the power of authority, empower another person to participate in the work of the congregation instead. This stops the abuse of positions and voting in another's name, which we consider to be positive and important. Article 18(2) changes and says: "The duty of the president of the tourist municipality or of the city and county shall be exercised by the municipal chief, mayor or mayor." An extremely important and major change in the law, which improves the co-operation of the tourist community and local self-government, has happened in practice that the presidents of tourist communities, representatives of tourism companies who have usually indirectly filtered the ideas of administration through tourist communities. They imposed costs, guidelines for working towards the tourist community. With this change, the tourist community will cooperate more effectively on the city's projects, assist in co-financing, joint promotion and destination management, which is listed in this new law as one of the new tasks of the tourist community. We had examples of where in tourist communities the presidents, i.e. The members and president of the council or the assembly of the tourist community are even a representative of the company that in the meantime became the owner of a foreign person, meaning a foreign company. So we have a president of the tourist community who represents a foreign company, practically, a tourist house, which I do not think that this company will certainly represent the legitimate and quality public interest of a city or municipality. So this change is very important. With the fact that it is likely that some amendments will go so that perhaps some person who can be authorised by the mayor, mayor, or possibly the mayor to be on his behalf. In Article 19(1) of the amendment to the law, one member of the Board of Supervisors of the Tourist Board of the Tourist Board delegates the tourism council of the County. This wants to carry out a monitoring that is impartial, even more quality, thus reducing the potential for poor management. In Article 23 of the Director of the Tourist Office, professional workers shall be required to carry out the tasks of the tourist community, except for the fulfilment of the specific conditions laid down in the Rules of Procedure, to have a professional examination passed, which has not been the case so far. Also, Article 23 discusses a conflict of interest, and in practice it was very often possible to find examples where the directors of tourist communities or members of their families perform a tourist activity directly related to the work of the tourist community. In that case, there was the possibility of agreeing to certain decisions. The legislator, precisely because of these problems in practice, wanted to end this negative development. Article 30, paragraph 1 as voluntary members, introduces one quality, one novelty to which members of the tourist community in accordance with Article 28. This is a request from persons who wish to enable prominent individuals who wish to participate in the work of the assembly but who do not have a high enough payment of the residence fee to be able to participate in the work of the assembly. Article 26 discusses the cooperation of tourist communities with the bodies of units of local self-government, municipalities and cities, the joint use of residence tax is agreed, the development of tourism development plans, master plans and the like. So there's a major shift towards co-operation with local self-government. In Article 32, in the list of tasks, the tourist community of course has some news under this law, as I say, management of tourist some, resources in terms of plans, master plans etc. In Article 33(4), it is considered as a share of the tourism community’s income, paragraph 3 of this Article, membership fees and 25% of the residence tax. So, the change is that a maximum of 1 member can have a third of the representatives in the congregation. ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-09-0.u22166	Mr. President of the Croatian Parliament, ladies and gentlemen of the Parliament, the honourable representatives of the motion, Mr. Minister. Three legislative proposals that are before this House, the Proposal for a Law on Tourist Communities and the Promotion of Croatian Tourism, the Proposal for a Law on residence tax, the Proposal for a Law on Memberships in Tourist Communities, are linked to each other so that they really form a functional and legal package that prescribes the establishment and operation of a system of tourist communities. This is necessary for their compliance, and this means, at the same time, changes and additions, which in this case, given the numerous changes so far and the addition of existing laws with the right to choose the form of new legislative proposals in order to avoid the transparency and incomprehensibility of this very important legislation. As so far, tourist communities are legal persons in whom concepts meet and co-operate in the public and private sectors for the purpose of developing, acting and promoting Croatian tourism as important economic activities in which they pursue their interests. Mandatory forms are, according to Article 4 of the new legal proposal, the tourist community of the municipality or city, the tourist community of the area in which the tourist communities of municipalities and cities are not established, the tourist community of the City of Zagreb, the tourist community of the county and the Croatian tourist community as a roof organisation for the territory of the Republic of Croatia. But mandatory tourist communities are possible, the law predicts them but with the previous consent of the ministry and provided that the means of operation are provided by the municipality or city from their budgets. Mandatory members are also bonds of payment of membership fees, which are legal and natural persons who generate income by providing services in tourism or with tourism directly related activities. The amount of membership fees was determined by the draft membership laws in tourist communities and these tourist resorts are classified as so far in four classes A, B, C and D, with all settlements above 30,000 inhabitants now in class A. The rate of the membership fee depends on the activity group and the tourist class and on the rate of the total income. The tax administration is empowered to supervise the payment of membership fees to legal and physical persons, and the Ministry of Oversight in tourist communities. The bodies of the tourist community are a congregation of the tourist community of the municipality, city, etc. while the Assembly at national level is called the National Assembly of the Tourist Board. The tourist council is the executive body of the tourist community, the supervisory board also exists under this law-proposed structure. The president of the tourist community is a special institution, and the paper is that the president of the tourist community is in office, so not so far as the selection of members of the assembly but as mayor, or mayor. These have avoided the unsolicited situations that have occurred so far. The duplication between local self-government and the assembly of the tourist community and the inability and choice and functioning that actually the entire system has been holding back. The activities of tourist communities and in this as well as in the current law are based on general utility, but this law proposes and brings the exception of possible commercial action, and the tourist community can manage tourism infrastructure, organise events and events, conduct commercial ads in its votes, give souvenirs, tourist maps and guides, intercede in booking accommodation if there is no travel agency in this place. There is also an obligation of professional examination for working in the tourist office for the director, as well as for professional workers. The residence tax law determines how payments, conditions and obligations of this most issued income for the activities of tourist communities. They've been detained from the law so far by a good solution, by a pause for private renters, and by a vignette for boaters. And here, after 14 years of functioning of the tourism community system, it has been shown that this system is a good, animator and promoter of Croatian tourism, but the shortcomings that have also been identified have also been shown and this legislative proposal has tried to address these shortcomings. I think the solutions are good. I support the adoption of this law and I also support the urgent procedure in order to show the improvements it brings already in this next tourist season.

ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-09-0.u22250	Thank you, Mr. Chairman. Once again, respect for the parliamentary representative and parliamentary representatives. In front of you is the proposal for a law on golf courses with the Final Bill. I must say that when the Ministry of Tourism was assigned to build a set of laws, then I am in this contribution to work for the term of office. Three are connected to tourist communities, one is tied to golf courses. What we wanted and what I wanted as Minister of Tourism was actually our task to do in the shortest period of time. Questions arise as to why golf is important for Croatian tourism and why the draft law is actually made in the tourism sector. So it is clear that as far as we are concerned in the Ministry of Tourism, we consider golfing content as part of the offer of Croatian tourism. I will stress this moment that we want, therefore, one more quality content, that the new quality of Croatian tourism, not only through the number of hotels, through raising quality, through new marinas, but also through the facilities that we lack, must be present in the tourist offer of the Republic of Croatia. For me personally, this law has the task of actually being a twin. It is one thing to remove the dilemma, a dilemma that no Croatian tourist who comes to Croatia should have. The dilemma is simple, at the moment when it comes to Croatia, it comes to a country that promotes high quality in tourist offer, but we are also a country that currently has virtually no golf course according to golf standards and it is clear that the dilemma that a tourist has is whether to come to Croatia for a tourist tour, vacation or for some other reason to choose Croatia's destination, but at the time of stay in Croatia does not play golf, or what is something we do not want to change the destination, so it is our potential tourist or somewhere where he can actually stay, but he can play golf. It is a dilemma that no tourist in Croatia should have. Of course, we have to watch where the competition is. I'll say that according to the data we have right now, our competition has about six golf courses per million arrivals of tourists. We have 11 million tourists, if we were in the standard we should have something over 60 golf courses, we're actually in a situation now that we don't have any. We even have a situation for people to come on vacation in Croatia, say in Dubrovnik, to bring their golf clubs and come to Dubrovnik for reception and simply ask where the golf course is. What are they asking? Because they mean that there is a golf course in Croatia. Of course we have to say or the receptionist at that hotel's reception that if they want to play golf, they have to go to another country. So right now, we've got 60 golf courses plotted in spatial plans. In the last 15 years, therefore, that possibility has existed, it has been indicated and I say in spatial plans on the county level. However, the situation is such that in Croatia, I would say the possibility, no one has taken advantage of it. I'm going to say that at this point of course we believe that obviously this investment cycle of investment in golf should be strengthened and this content created in as short a time as possible. I will not compare Croatia only with destinations that are traditionally strong, so one Italy or one Spanish, but also those countries that were with us in a certain environment, such as one Czech Republic, one Slovakia that is not competitive in the right sense of that word, but now have 20 and more golf courses, and that they clearly attract a special and quality population that is absolutely of paramount importance to us in tourism. The law itself is therefore extremely short, it has 16 articles and I will say that this law actually determines what is most important that the law can be asked at all as such, and that is the question whether golf is of interest to the Republic of Croatia or not. The law clearly states that the law is of interest, that golf is of interest to the Republic of Croatia and based on it are actually created a series of legal provisions that should be made for those investors who are already deep in the practical construction phase for which there are a number of preconditions, to enable them to use opportunities to base the fact that it is of interest to the Republic of Croatia, the law could shape this plot and actually go towards me to tell the possibility of building a golf course. The problem that always exists is the problem of large areas needed for golf courses and therefore, of course, in these legislative frameworks, it is envisaged that in some situations we simply allow but only in exceptional situations that actually whoever is the investor, who has the prerequisites, can therefore form a building plot. I'm going to say what we're changing here in no way, so we don't just have to indicate what we're going to change, but what remains unquestionable. This law does not change environmental standards. So, right now, Croatia and this law are predominant that nature protection will be at the highest level. Only the golf course is not in contradiction with nature protection. I will say that one Switzerland, one Austria, one Sweden have a series of golf courses that we can't say for sure now, those countries are countries with low standards, but the law does not bypass any procedures that are related to that segment. The second thing, we continue to leave the local, and in particular the regional self-government the possibility that is most important, and that is the possibility that through spatial plans determine what is most important, how much golf courses should have, whether they are suitable for me to say construction and determine how much the ratios are actually related to the buildings on the golf course and only the golf course. We do not impose here but limit the possibility of therefore building on golf courses so that the provisions here that have been established determine which are the maximum, and these maximums continue to determine therefore the units or regional self-government. We are also not going to this law to any apartmentisation, the reason simple, golf courses are certainly neither the end of the sea nor are they located in certain areas of such nature to make apartments there. It is clear that they are located in areas that are suitable for that, which are usually in the background and which actually enable this area to be ecologically protected, and I would also say in what we want, a high economic value. And at the end of the golf courses of course serve us, I will especially emphasize that, for attracting those tourists who are high paying powers and later I would say that golf is not related to the main season. So golfers and golf courses are used most just when Croatia needs them most, and this is in the pre-season and season and this is a special value and convenience which is therefore related to this content. With this law, in fact, Croatia, too, so I would say the Ministry of Tourism says we want investors, but we don't want investors in everything. We don't want investors in apartments, we don't want investors I'd say in what we think Croatian tourism is not necessary but we actually want investors in the high quality content that Croatia lacks, and one of these contents is golf courses. Thank you, Mr. Chairman.

The promoter is a public interest found in the argument that tourism is a strategic economic activity for Croatia, that it is in our exceptional interest to extend the tourist season in Croatia, and the magic wand that will enable it is precisely mass construction of golf courses throughout Croatia without any strategy, plan and order. The spacious plans of our cities and municipalities provide for the construction of as many as 60 golf courses in Croatia, which is the number that this bill is also experiencing, when it provides us with information on an average of 6 to 7 golf courses per million tourists, which is reportedly the average in tourist and Mediterranean countries. If we apply this to Croatia, which was reportedly visited by some 11 million tourists in 2007, we come to a famous 60 golf course number. Where? Well, you know. in attractive coastal Adriatic areas, in karst and just along the coast of the sea. And how much construction of 60 golf courses according to this law will have facilities of exceptional importance for the Republic of Croatia with negative effects on the environment, this bill does not speak in general. And precisely the negative effects on the environment and nature are the main reasons why the European Union countries have significantly tempered their enthusiasm for building new golf courses over the past few years. Because golf courses require extremely much irrigation, maintenance of monocultural grass, requires the use of large quantities of various chemicals, fertilisers, hebricids and pesticides that systematically poison water, especially extremely valuable groundwater in karst areas, and permanently pollute the soil. Should it be recalled that the towns and municipalities on the Adriatic coast, already today have extremely big problems with water supply in the summer months, when a large number of eco-aware and needed tourists travel to relatively small areas in the already famous manner of mass tourism a la Cro. What happens to water supply in these parts if we build that number of golf courses? The reasons why I object to this bill are not only motivated by the desire to protect and preserve the environment, but also by the desire to preserve space as extremely valuable, I would say the most valuable Croatian resource, especially when we talk about an attractive coastal area. And the indisputable fact is that this bill puts that space at risk. Namely, this law is just incredibly liberal in terms of the construction and sale of apartments and villas that are clearly planned to be built massively within the golf course and on the surface of 25% of the total floor area of the playground. Since the promoter predicts that the golf course must have a minimum of 85 hectares and is not in line with the practice of other countries where the 18-hole playgrounds meet in some 60 hectares, this means that it is allowed to build apartments and other accompanying facilities in almost 20 hectares, which is a very large building area in extremely attractive areas. In other words, welcome to concreteisation and apartmentisation in Croatian way, or bad news for Croatian space but excellent news for investors of hungry profits. For these investors, the hungry profit of this bill is being handled by another precisely incredible provision of this bill. Those are the provisions concerning the expropriation. Article 9 stipulates that an exemption is allowed for the purpose of building a golf course up to 20% of the total area of the playground and that is very, very much again. In this regard, I think it is necessary to remind myself that the expropriation measures are used to realise extremely important projects of public interest such as infrastructure corridors, roads, highways and golf courses, at least in a world where I live are not objects of such public interest. Why? Because golf courses are used by a relatively small number of people mostly by higher payload capacity because the realisation of golf courses is an investment, spatial and environmentally invasive and added value that the social community can have from the realization of such projects is extremely small. In this regard, I conclude that realising golf courses is particularly harmful to the interests of the wider social community in Croatia. Or in other words, big investments, minimal benefits. A large number of people are not employed on golf courses, which would potentially benefit the local community and resource consumption is enormous. In the law, the proposer suggests that the revenues from golf tourism are large, so I guess the scale of the profit scale should outweigh the damage to the environment and space on the other side of the scale. However, when asked on the committee, how many incomes so far and the economic benefits of existing golf courses in Brijuni and Krasic, and in Blato, the promoter did not answer that question. We can only question why, whether income is negligible or because there's no record of it. I don't know which of these two variants is worse because one means knowingly and horrendous fraud and betraying public interests, and the other involves the incredible unpreparedness of the competent executive authorities who are flying into a mass golf project like geese in the fog. After all I've said before, I have to ask this question. Who is in the interest of this law at all, because this law is definitely not in the interest of Croatian citizens and citizens and is not in the interest of Croatian tourism or Croatian economy. I think that it is clear to all of us today who are discussing this law that this law is in the exclusive interest of large-scale capital, especially golf investors and construction lobbys, which, through this law, are wide open to the possibility of simple concreteisation and apartmentization of extremely valuable space, with the whole-hearted assistance of state structures. But the work of state structures is not to defend the particular interests of narrow mostly elite lobbys but to defend the public interest of all Croatian citizens and citizens. Therefore, dear colleagues and colleagues, I appeal to your conscience and invite you not to support this immoral and unfair bill.

ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-09-0.u22332	I thank you, Mr. Vice President, given that my colleague Verbat said something that has nothing to do with reality, the truth. Behind that went "cha" as you can see, right. In his debate, a mustache of pears and apples. Here is a modest experience in the local self-government that lasts for 14 years that units of local self-government bring space-planning documentation and based and spatial planning documents make plans for what will be done in a given space. But it means it's not a matter of space-planned documentation, it's a matter of investor's party colors. But regardless, I will say something first about the history of golf in the Republic of Croatia. Golf is a sports and recreation game that takes place in nature and whose specific features are the design of a playground with landscape structures. It was created in Scotland in the 14th century on natural grasslands by the sea, and the first human-shaped playgrounds appeared in the 18th century. The exceptional popularity of golf can thank the basic elements of the game such as: competitive features, strategy, quick decision-making and exercise. It is interesting to mention that golf rules have been unchanged for centuries, which gives the game a romantic and traditional tone. The history of golf in the Republic of Croatia dates back to World War II when golf was played at three locations. On the island of Veli Brijun, in Zagreb and in Ičići between Opatija and Lovran. Unfortunately, from the end of the '30s to the early '90s, golf was totally dead. At the beginning of the 90s, a group of enthusiasts restarted golf in Croatia. Golf began to play on Brijuni in our neighborhoods in the half of the 19th century. The Brijuni Islands was bought by Austrian industrialist Paul Cooperweiser and turned them into a tourist mecca. During the first half of the 20th century, numerous sports facilities are offered in Brijuni, such as water sports, crickets, tennis, horseback riding, and golf has been included since 1922. This year, a golf course with 18 fields was completed. According to the press, it was one of the most beautiful golf courses in Europe. It is not hard to believe when the natural beauty of that landscape is considered. The first official tournament was held next year. The appearance of the playground is not known much but there is evidence that in the early 30s the length of the playground was 4000 450 yards. According to some sources, the first attempt to start a golf club in Zagreb was in 1925, but there are documents according to which the City Department then refused to give consent to the establishment of the club. The re-launching of the club was successfully conducted in 1929 and the founders of the Zagreb Golf Club were the most visible persons of the then Zagreb led by Count Miroslav Kulmer. The founder of the club was also the Esplanada Hotel in which the club headquarters was located. In 1931, the club had 80 members, and already in 1932 the club numbers about 102 members. The number of members for the then Zagreb was indeed respectable. By the beginning of 1931, a 9-field playground was built. The playground is solemnly opened on June 12th 1931 with the presence of many dignitaries. In Ičići, a coastal place halfway between Opatija and Lovran on the terrace slopes of Učka in May 1930 is an open golf course. It was built on the land of the Tomasic family... And what was most characteristic of this playground were beautiful and unforgettable views that shot at Kvarner from each of the 9 fields. [[The speaker doesn't understand.]] The warm climate and way of building allowed the game throughout the year, which was mentioned with beautiful views as the greatest grace of this golf course that was advertised as golf in the mountains on the coast of the sea. The playground was used, this is information for Mr. Caine even though he wasn't here, so the playground was used until 1943 to capitulate Italy when German military forces came to the area. In two key places, concrete bunkers were built on the playground, and the devastation of the playground began. After World War II, no less was founded than a nursery. And later, the spaces are used as a camp to this day. The golf course in Croatia starts only in the last decade of the 20th century after a long break. Despite the war, groups of enthusiasts are launching the establishment of several clubs in Zagreb and Sinj. In addition to the 200x300m swagger, members of the Bundek Golf Club organize the first exercise site. The second group of enthusiasts organizes a practice area between Horvacan road and the Faculty of Kinesiology. The Croatian Golf Federation was founded in June 1992. All 3 months later, the Croatian Golf Federation was admitted to the European Golf Association and a few years later a professional golf association was registered. Today, more than 60 million are playing golf in the world, and more than 7 million in Europe. Golf and golf tourism are in a strong trend of growth as more and more middle and younger people turn to golf as an ideal form of individual and family sports recreation and rest. New generations of golfers are mostly well-sighted family people who spend significantly more on golf and golf tourism than on average tourists. Western Mediterranean countries have an average of 6 golf courses per million arrivals of tourists. Example Portugal 6, Spain 5, France 7, Italy 6. In Croatia, the number is 20 times smaller which speaks eloquently about the size of our untapped potential for the development of golf tourism. Research shows that every other golfer goes on vacation, and every third one goes every year. Croatia as a potential golf destination in its nearer environment such as Slovenia, Italy and Austria because of the concept, which due to winter has no real competition. In addition to Western Mediterranean countries, Turkey has expanded as a new golf destination in the eastern Mediterranean. There are currently two 18-hole golf courses in Croatia and one 9-hole exercise area that certainly does not meet the needs of our tourist offer by number or quality. The visions of golf prosperity and its contribution are linked to economic development, environmental protection, social improvement and the concept of sustainability in golf. In order to recognise what is needed for the development of golf and a given social environment, it is necessary to take account of integrated management of the environment, economic development and human well-being. Not just in today's time frames, but for future generations. Golf is certainly the most healthy sport that young and old people can play without age. A large number of playgrounds, their availability and sometimes integration into the school system affect its popularity and acceptability. Golf has turned from individual sports to a family sport that all members enjoy equally. Golf can be a good investment in health and although it may not belong to the category of most energetic sports or high-intensity sports, but according to new research it can have a very positive impact on your health and even enable you to live a long life. The aim of researchers from Sweden was one of the largest European medical institutes to study the health status of 300,000 golfers, and announced that the incidence of deaths in them was 40% lower than in people of the same age, gender and socioeconomic situation. In other words, golf allowed them another five years to live. Professor Alba, the leader of the research team, explains this phenomenon by the fact that there are several segments in golf that have a positive impact on health. Thank you.

The same applies to part of the provision that prescribes that they are an integral part of the golf course and other accompanying facilities.

ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-09-0.u22489	Thank you very much, Vice President. Dear ladies and gentlemen, the Law on State Support for Education and Education of the Croatian Parliament adopted in October 2007 and the said law prescribes financial support as tax relief for entrepreneurs who refer to their workers and entrepreneurs whose plants and workshops are trained in craftsmanship. The law lays down that when calculating state support for education and education, the basics of profit and income tax are reduced by a prescribed percentage of the eligible costs incurred in relation to education and education. However, according to the Income Tax Act, the total annual base of income tax is determined on the basis of the income which natural persons can obtain from non-permanent labour income, independent activity, property and property rights, capital, insurance and other income, and the aim of the legislator was to provide tax relief for the education and education of taxpayers to taxpayers who earn income only from independent activity, which is the existing State Support Act to be brought into line with the provisions of the Income Tax Act. This means that, as a basis for the calculation of support for education and education for individuals performing economic activity, income from independent activity rather than the basis for the calculation of income tax is to be determined. Furthermore, the Law on State Support for Education and Education also provides for support for entrepreneurs whose plants and workshops are subject to training for crafts activities and, according to the provisions of the law, the maximum amount of support is fixed in percentage, only in the percentage amount of the reduction of the base of the accounts, and therefore, due to the high amount of profits generated by the largest companies, it is necessary to limit the amount of support to an absolute amount, i.e. to the amount prescribed by the provisions of the Income Tax Regulation. These shortcomings and discrepancies with the provisions of the Income Tax Act were corrected by the Regulation amending and supplementing the State Support Act adopted by the Government of the Republic of Croatia at a session held in December 2007. Furthermore, following the adoption of the laws and regulations, the Government of the Republic of Croatia also made a decision on a map of regional support which sets out areas in which state support to economic development can be granted, and with this decision it is necessary to comply with the provisions of the Law on State Support for Education and Education. With regard to this proposal for a law, it is proposed that natural persons carrying out an economic activity should be determined to reduce the income of independent activity, rather than the basis for the calculation of the income tax when calculating state support for education and education. Also, according to the rules on income taxation, i.e. in accordance with Article 45(1), paragraph 1 is replaced by the following: The Income Tax Regulation shall determine the maximum amount of support available to scientists by entrepreneurs whose plants and workshops are subject to training for craftsmanship, which is now 1600 kn per scientist per month and so that the users of state support for education, education are allowed to reduce the base of profit tax and income from independent activity to 10 percentage points, and if the activity is carried out in the area defined as the area of central and eastern and Adriatic Croatia by decision on the map of regional support, i.e. 5 percentage points, respectively, if the activity is carried out in the area of northwestern Croatia. Therefore, the amendments to this law are done to comply with the Income Tax Act, the Income Tax Regulation and the same with the regional support map. Ladies and gentlemen, I hope you will accept this Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on State Support for Education and Education, and I would also like to say that we accept the amendment of the Committee on Legislatives.
ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-09-0.u22490	Thank you. Committees: legislation, development and reconstruction. Committee of Education, Science and Culture Mr Nevio Šetić. Here you go.
ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-09-0.u22491	Your Honor, Mr. Vice President, ladies and gentlemen of the Parliament, the Honorable Secretary of State. The Committee on Education, Science and Culture of the Croatian Parliament discussed it at its 10th session on 4 December 2008. Proposal for a Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on State Support for Education and Education with the Final Proposal for a Law submitted by the Government of the Republic of Croatia to the President of the Croatian Parliament by the Act of 27 November 2008. The Committee discussed the issue as an interested working body. The opening argument was tabled by the applicant's representative. The draft law corrects the current Decision of the Income Tax Act which does not adequately address the question of the Law on State Support for the Education and Education of Workers, i.e. its provisions on financial support as tax relief for entrepreneurs who refer to their workers and entrepreneurs whose facilities and workshops are trained in craftsmanship. As regards the first one, the applicant points out that the issue should be brought into line with the Income Tax Act and so that, as a basis for calculating support for education, education for natural persons carrying out economic activity, income from independent activity should be determined, but not the basis for the calculation of income tax as is the case now. As regards the other issues, the proposal for a law lays down a new solution to restricting the reduction of income tax base or the base of profit tax for entrepreneurs whose plants and workshops are subject to training for craft professions. Since, under the existing regulation, larger companies can, on this basis, be released today in unwarranted absolute amounts, all within the limit to 15% of the tax base. The proposal proposes the adoption of an emergency law, since it considers that this requires particularly justified state reasons by calling for Article 159. Rules of Procedure of the Croatian Parliament. Because it considers that the obligations of large employers, if not limited in absolute terms, can lead to major economic disturbances in the application of those rules. Without discussion, the committee unanimously concluded. It is proposed to the Croatian Parliament to pass the Law on State Support for Education and Education.

ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-09-0.u22493	Thank you. Mr. Vice President, Madam Secretary of State, distinguished colleagues and colleagues. At the beginning of the debate on the Proposal for a Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on State Support for Education and Education, I would like to say that the Croatian Democratic Community Club will support that law in the urgent process of the united first and second readings, as proposed by the Government of the Republic of Croatia. The Law on State Support for Education and Education of Workers adopted the Croatian Parliament in October 2007. The main reasons were the rapid development of technology, and the growing competition of economic operators in the international market required the permanent education of workers, lifelong education and training of workers, except for the positive effects of business, of course, and the increase in the cost of employers. As a result, the Law on State Support for Education and Education of Workers provides financial support as a tax relief to entrepreneurs who refer their workers to education and education, and to entrepreneurs whose plants and workshops are trained in craftsmanship. This Law amending and supplementing the Law on State Support for Education and Education seeks to bring the provisions of the existing law in line with the provisions of the Income Tax Act, Decisions on a map of regional support, and to limit, in absolute terms, the support that entrepreneurs can achieve for receiving scientists to learn and propose: For natural persons carrying out economic activity, determine that when calculating state support for education and education, income from independent activity is reduced, rather than the basis for the calculation of income tax as prescribed by the provisions of the law. Two. To determine the maximum amount of support that a scientist can provide for entrepreneurs whose facilities and workshops are trained for craft professions in accordance with the untaxable amount of rewards to students during practical work and teaching that are harmonised in accordance with the rules on income taxation. And lastly, the users of state support for education, education allow for a reduction in the base of profit tax and income from independent activity to increase by 10 percentage points, if the activity is carried out in the area defined as the area of central eastern, i.e. the Pannonian Croatia, i.e. up to 5 percentage points if the activity is carried out in the area of northwestern Croatia. By reducing the income from independent activity in the calculation of support for education and education of the provisions of the existing Law on State Support for Education and Education, it will be brought into line with the provisions of the Income Tax Act, and by prescribing the maximum possible amount of support that can be achieved by entrepreneurs whose workshops and plants will remove unreal high amounts that can be achieved by the largest entrepreneurs in the Republic of Croatia under the existing provisions of the law. Furthermore, this will level the amount of support that scientists can receive for teaching. big and small entrepreneurs. In order to implement this law, there is no need to provide additional resources from the budget of the Republic of Croatia, and we have received the opinion of the Competition Protection Agency, which says that the amendments and additions to the Law on State Support for Education and Education contained in the draft Bill on State Support for Education and Education are made in accordance with the provisions of the State Support Act. Because of everything I said, the Croatian Democratic Community club supports the adoption of the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on State Support for Education and Education. Thank you.
